**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

The relevant spatial propositions are:
- **Prop 2:** The umbrella is at a distance from the victim's body
- **Prop 7:** The victim fell on top of his umbrella

**Step 3:** Explain how these propositions reveal the contradiction

These two propositions directly contradict each other. If the umbrella is at a distance from the victim's body (as shown in Crime Photo 1), then the victim could not have fallen on top of his umbrella. Conversely, if the victim fell on top of his umbrella (as stated in Phoenix's testimony), then the umbrella would be underneath the victim's body, not at a distance from it.

**Step 4:** This contradiction is explicitly revealed by the spatial propositions listed above.

Lang's testimony claims there's no other way for the president to get down from the roof without using the elevator (Testimony 3). However, the Bigg Building evidence (Evidence 2) reveals there is an entrance to a hidden fifty-first floor on the roof, which provides an alternative vertical path that doesn't involve the elevator.

Prop 3 states: "The elevator is the only vertical path between the roof and ground floor of the Bigg Building." This directly relates to the contradiction.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Prop 3 claims the elevator is the ONLY vertical path, but Evidence 2 reveals there's a hidden entrance to the 51st floor on the roof. This creates a contradiction because the existence of this hidden entrance means the elevator is NOT the only vertical path available, contrary to what Lang asserts in Testimony 3.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Not applicable - Prop 3 directly applies to this contradiction.

When Simeon is above the rooftop in a balloon, the body double on the rooftop below cannot be "in front of" him in a way that allows pointing a gun directly at him. **Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

The spatial propositions about the car accident are:
- Prop 1: Ini was in the passenger seat
- Prop 3: The driver's seat is to the left of the passenger seat in Mimi's English sports car
- Prop 5: Ini exited through the right door after the crash

**Step 3:** Apply the propositions to reveal the contradiction

In an English sports car (which has right-hand drive), the driver's seat is on the right side of the car, and the passenger seat is on the left. However, Prop 3 states the driver's seat is to the left of the passenger seat, which would only be true in a left-hand drive car (like American cars), not an English right-hand drive car.

This creates a contradiction: If the car is English (Testimony 3), then the driver's seat should be on the right, making the passenger seat on the left. But Prop 3 claims the driver's seat is left of the passenger seat, which contradicts the English car configuration.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning shows that Testimony 3 ("a totally special model from England") directly contradicts the spatial arrangement described in the propositions, as English cars have right-hand drive.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at Phoenix's testimony (Testimony 3), he states: "After I shoved him, he... He fell down on top of his cheap umbrella." This suggests the victim fell directly onto an umbrella that was already on the ground.

However, examining Crime Photo 1 (Evidence 2), the description states: "An umbrella with a bent handle lies open in a distance." This indicates the umbrella was not directly beneath the victim when he fell, but rather positioned at some distance away.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Several propositions are relevant:
- Prop 2: The umbrella is to the right of the victim's body in Crime Photo 1
- Prop 4: The victim fell downward onto the umbrella that was on the ground
- Prop 5: The victim's body is covering the umbrella after the fall
- Prop 7: The umbrella is near the electrical pole at the crime scene

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Prop 2 directly contradicts Phoenix's testimony. If the umbrella is "to the right of the victim's body" in the crime photo, this means the victim could not have fallen directly "on top of" the umbrella as Phoenix claims. The spatial relationship shows the umbrella was positioned separately from where the victim landed, not underneath him.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

N/A - Proposition 2 applies directly.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format

{"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

- Testimony 3: "The camera was set up to take pictures of a meteor shower."

The spatial propositions show the camera is positioned facing the lake (Props 1, 2, 5, 10), which is a horizontal orientation. However, to photograph a meteor shower, a camera would need to be angled upward toward the sky, not horizontally facing the lake. This creates a spatial contradiction - the camera's actual positioning (facing the lake) is incompatible with its stated purpose (photographing meteors in the sky).

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

However, in Testimony 5, Andrews states: "I was the one who knocked the flower vase over, where it fell onto the guitar case." This indicates Andrews altered the crime scene by knocking over the vase.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

- Prop 3: The smashed vase is on the floor next to the guitar case
- Prop 4: The water from the smashed vase is on top of the guitar case lid

These propositions describe the vase as already smashed and on the floor, with water on the guitar case - which matches the crime scene photo. However, Andrews claims she knocked the vase over onto the guitar case, implying she caused this arrangement.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Propositions 3 and 4 reveal the contradiction. The crime scene photo (and thus the spatial propositions) shows the vase already smashed on the floor with water on the guitar case. However, Andrews' testimony that she "knocked the flower vase over" suggests she caused this arrangement, which contradicts her earlier testimony that she saw "the exact same scene as in the crime scene photo" - because if she knocked the vase over, the scene she initially saw would have been different from the final crime scene photo.

**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at Kay's testimonies, particularly Testimony 3: "But... from under the cherry tree... I feel like... I saw something red." This suggests Kay was positioned under the cherry tree when she saw something red.

However, looking at the spatial propositions and evidence, we know:
- Prop 3: The cherry tree is to the left of the candy stand on the viewing platform
- Prop 4: Kay was on the viewing platform when she was pushed
- Prop 5: The person who pushed Kay came from beyond the candy stand
- Testimony 4: "someone was walking towards me from the other side of that stand"

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

The key spatial relationship is that the cherry tree and candy stand are on opposite sides of the viewing platform (left and right respectively). If Kay was under the cherry tree (left side), then someone coming from beyond the candy stand would be coming from the right side.

**Step 3:** The contradiction lies between Testimony 3 and Testimony 4

Testimony 3 suggests Kay was under the cherry tree (left side), but Testimony 4 states someone was walking towards her from beyond the candy stand (right side). However, this isn't actually a contradiction - these statements are compatible.

Looking more carefully, the actual contradiction is between **Testimony 3** and **Evidence 8 (Kay's Recollections)**. Evidence 8 states Kay remembers "a person coming from beyond the concession stand and pushing her off of the building," which aligns with Testimony 4. However, Testimony 3 says she saw something red "from under the cherry tree," which creates a spatial inconsistency with her position when pushed.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning reveals that if Kay was pushed from beyond the candy stand (right side), she couldn't have been under the cherry tree (left side) at the moment she was pushed, as these are opposite ends of the platform.

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 8, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at Ini's testimonies, particularly Testimony 3: "And then... like, I took my sister to the Channeling Chamber." This implies Ini was physically present and walking with her sister to the Channeling Chamber.

However, examining the evidence, we have Evidence 1: "Guidemap (Fey Manor)" which shows that to get from the Side Room to the Channeling Chamber, one must go through a "winding path" from the meditation room. Additionally, Evidence 14: "Sacred Urn" indicates the urn was "under repair in the hallway until police came."

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

Looking at the spatial propositions:
- Prop 3: "The hallway connects the Side Room to the Channeling Chamber"
- Prop 4: "The Sacred Urn was located in the hallway when it was under repair"

**Step 3:** Apply relevant propositions to reveal the contradiction

According to Prop 3, the hallway connects the Side Room to the Channeling Chamber. Prop 4 states the Sacred Urn was in the hallway during repair. This means anyone traveling from the Side Room to the Channeling Chamber would necessarily pass through the hallway where the Sacred Urn was located.

However, Ini claims in Testimony 4: "I, like, didn't see anyone on the way to, like, the Channeling Chamber." This creates a contradiction because if Ini was truly walking her sister to the Channeling Chamber, she would have had to pass through the hallway where the Sacred Urn was being repaired, yet she claims she saw no one.

**Step 4:** Spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial layout shows that the only path from the Side Room to the Channeling Chamber goes through the hallway (Prop 3). The Sacred Urn was in this hallway during repair (Prop 4), which would have been a notable obstruction or at least visible to anyone passing through. Ini's claim of not seeing anyone contradicts the physical necessity of passing through this space.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the Witness's Photo (Evidence 6), it shows the victim standing close to the broken east end of Dusky Bridge, facing west toward Fawles. According to Melissa's testimony (Testimony 3), she took the photo at the "crucial moment" when they started fighting.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

Prop 3 states: "The victim is standing close to the broken east end of Dusky Bridge"
Prop 9 states: "The victim runs approximately 10 yards west from her position before being stabbed"

**Step 3:** Apply the propositions to reveal the contradiction

The Witness's Photo (Evidence 6) shows the victim at the broken east end of the bridge. However, according to Testimony 6, the victim "only got about 10 yards before she was stabbed in the back." This creates a spatial contradiction because:

- If the victim was already at the broken east end (as shown in the photo), she couldn't have run 10 yards west - she would have immediately fallen off the bridge
- The broken east end means there's no bridge surface beyond that point to run on
- Therefore, the photo showing the victim at the broken edge contradicts the testimony that she ran 10 yards west before being stabbed

**Step 4:** The spatial propositions clearly establish this contradiction

Prop 3 confirms the victim's position at the broken east end in the photo, while Prop 9 describes her running 10 yards west - an impossible movement from that location.

Looking at Victor Kudo's testimonies, he describes the victim (Glen Elg) as having a newspaper in his right hand (Testimony 1) and picking up the coffee cup with his free hand (Testimony 4). This implies the victim was holding the newspaper in his right hand and using his left hand to pick up the cup.

However, examining the Coffee Cup evidence (Evidence 13), we see that when facing the cup, the handle is to the right and the lip stain is on the front. This indicates the cup was positioned with the handle on the right side when viewed from the victim's perspective.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

Proposition 6 states: "The coffee cup's handle is to the right when facing the cup"
Proposition 7 states: "The lip stain is on the front of the coffee cup"

These propositions establish the cup's orientation - with the handle on the right side when viewed from the front.

**Step 3:** Explain how the propositions reveal the contradiction

Given the cup's handle is on the right (Proposition 6), a right-handed person would naturally pick up the cup with their right hand. However, Kudo's testimony states the victim was holding a newspaper in his right hand (Testimony 1) and used his free (left) hand to pick up the cup (Testimony 4). This creates a spatial contradiction - if the victim used his left hand to pick up a cup with the handle on the right, it would be an awkward and unnatural motion.

**Step 4:** The spatial propositions directly support identifying this contradiction by establishing the cup's orientation, which conflicts with the described hand usage in the testimonies.

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

- Prop 2: Mr. LeTouse's body is behind items that block Lamiroir's view from the entrance door
- Prop 6: Two bullet holes are on the wall where the entrance door is located
- Prop 10: The wall with bullet holes is behind Mr. LeTouse's body from Lamiroir's perspective at the entrance door

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

The contradiction is revealed by Props 2, 6, and 10. According to these propositions:
- The bullet holes are on the wall where the entrance door is located (Prop 6)
- This wall is behind Mr. LeTouse's body from Lamiroir's perspective (Prop 10)
- Mr. LeTouse's body is blocked from view by items (Prop 2)

Lamiroir claims she "saw nothing" because the body was "behind a wall," but she also claims she "saw bullet holes in the wall." This is contradictory because if the body was behind items that blocked her view (as stated in the spatial propositions), she shouldn't have been able to see the bullet holes on the wall behind the body from her position at the entrance door.

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the evidence and testimonies, we need to find a contradiction between what is physically shown in the evidence versus what is claimed in the testimonies.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Examining the spatial propositions, we can see that Prop 6 and Prop 7 describe the exact card positions on the table. These propositions show:
- Near side: 7 heart, 7 diamond, 7 club, Ace spade, and Ace diamond (three 7s and two Aces = full house)
- Far side: King heart, King diamond, King spade, Ace club, and Ace heart (three Kings and two Aces = full house)

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Prop 6 and Prop 7 reveal that both players had full houses. However, when we look at Testimony 3 from Olga, she states "The one who was winning... dah, it was victim!" This creates a contradiction because:

- Both players have full houses (as shown by the spatial arrangement of cards in Props 6 and 7)
- A full house is a very strong poker hand
- With both players having full houses, the winner would be determined by the rank of the three-of-a-kind
- The victim has three Kings (higher than three 7s), so the victim would indeed win
- However, the contradiction lies in the chip distribution shown in Evidence 6 (Chip Photo), which shows the victim had significantly more chips (2 red + 9 gray = 9,200 points) versus defendant's chips (4 red + 1 gray = 1,400 points), suggesting the victim was already winning by a large margin before this final hand

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial propositions do apply here, as they establish the exact card positions that show both players had full houses, which supports the testimony that the victim was winning. However, the contradiction emerges when we consider that if the victim was already winning by such a large margin (as shown by the chip distribution), it seems unlikely they would risk everything on a single hand where both players had full houses.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format

{"evidence": 6, "testimony": 3}

--------------------
{
  "evidence": 6,
  "testimony": 3

- Prop 7 shows Windibank's body was found face down on the storeroom floor
- Evidence 12 (Windibank's Gun) indicates Gina was holding Windibank's gun when found unconscious

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

Prop 4 and Prop 7 are directly relevant - they establish that Windibank was shot from behind while facing away from the shooter, which contradicts Nash's testimony that Windibank was holding a gun and could have fired it.

**Step 3:** Explain how the propositions reveal the contradiction

Prop 4 (bullet entered from back while facing away) and Prop 7 (body found face down) make it impossible for Windibank to have been holding his gun at the time of the shooting, as he would have been facing away from the shooter and unable to fire. This directly contradicts Nash's claim that Windibank was holding a gun and could have fired it.

- Prop 10: The keycard that grants entry to the Committee chamber was found in the victim's hand inside the P.I.C. meeting room

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

The contradiction lies between Prop 5 and the testimonies. - The victim's cards (King heart, King diamond, King spade, Ace club, and Ace heart) are on the far side
- The defendant's cards (7 heart, 7 diamond, 7 club, Ace spade, and Ace diamond) are on the near side

Both hands are full houses, but the victim's hand (three Kings and two Aces) beats the defendant's hand (three 7s and two Aces). This appears consistent with the testimony.

However, looking more carefully at the chip distribution in Evidence 6:
- Near side (defendant): 4 red small chips (100 points each) + 1 gray big chip (1,000 points) = 1,400 points
- Far side (victim): 2 red small chips (100 points each) + 9 gray big chips (1,000 points each) = 9,200 points

This shows the victim had significantly more chips (9,200 vs 1,400), which contradicts Testimony 0 that states "The game began with 3,500 point in chips for each man." The spatial evidence shows an unequal distribution that cannot be explained by the testimony.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning reveals that the chip distribution shown in Evidence 6 directly contradicts the equal starting chip amounts claimed in Testimony 0.

{"evidence": 6, "testimony": 0}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the testimonies, Nash claims in Testimony 3 that "the bloke wot owned the place was 'oldin' a gun, so 'e should 'ave just fired instead of yellin' at the girl!" This suggests Windibank was holding a gun and could have fired it.

However, examining the spatial propositions and evidence:
- Prop 4 states the bullet entered Windibank's back while he was facing away from the shooter
- Prop 7 shows Windibank's body was found face down
- Evidence 12 (Windibank's Gun) was found being held by Gina when she was discovered unconscious

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

Prop 4 and Prop 7 are directly relevant - they establish that Windibank was shot from behind while facing away from the shooter, which contradicts Nash's testimony that Windibank was holding a gun and could have fired it.

**Step 3:** Explain how the propositions reveal the contradiction

Prop 4 and Prop 7 reveal that Windibank was facing away from the shooter when shot, making it impossible for him to have been holding his gun in a position to fire at the time of the shooting. This directly contradicts Nash's testimony that Windibank was holding a gun and should have fired it.

**Step 4:** Not applicable - the propositions do apply

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at Lang's testimonies, particularly Testimony 3 which states "Inside that monster costume over there..." and Testimony 4 which mentions "There's dirt inside the costume. It must have gotten there when the body was hidden inside."

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Examining the spatial propositions:
- Prop 1: President Wang's body is inside the monster costume
- Prop 4: The dirt inside the monster costume is below President Wang's body
- Prop 5: The body is covered by the monster costume

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

The contradiction lies between Testimony 4 and the spatial arrangement described in the propositions. Testimony 4 claims the dirt got inside the costume when the body was hidden there, but Prop 4 states the dirt is *below* President Wang's body. This creates a spatial impossibility - if the body was placed inside the costume, the dirt would be on top of or mixed with the body, not systematically below it as if the body was placed on top of pre-existing dirt.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial arrangement described in Prop 4 (dirt below the body) contradicts Lang's testimony that the dirt got there "when the body was hidden inside." If the body was placed inside the costume, the dirt would have to be disturbed and mixed with the body, not remain neatly below it.

This creates a contradiction with Testimony 4: "I, like, didn't see anyone on the way to, like, the Channeling Chamber." - Evidence 9 (Victim's Hand): Shows a full house with K♥, K♦, K♠, A♣, A♥

- Prop 8 states "The defendant's hand (a full house) was left at the scene on the table"
- Prop 9 states "The victim's hand (a full house) was left at the scene on the table"

These propositions confirm the hands were physically present at the scene, but don't specify their contents.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction lies in the card composition. Olga's testimony claims there are five aces total (three in Smith's hand and two in Wright's hand), but the actual evidence shows:
- Smith's hand contains only two aces (A♣, A♥)
- Wright's hand contains only two aces (A♠, A♦)
- This totals four aces, not five as claimed

This directly contradicts Olga's testimony about the number of aces present in the hands.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at Shih-na's testimonies:
- Testimony 1: She was searching for Yatagarasu during the second fire
- Testimony 2: She heard a scream from the next room over
- Testimony 3: She was in the next room but couldn't see Yatagarasu

Looking at the evidence:
- Evidence 29: "There are signs that the revolving fireplace wall on Babahl's side has been used. Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall and into the next room over."

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

The spatial propositions establish:
- Prop 3: The revolving fireplace wall connects two adjacent rooms
- Prop 4: Yatagarasu escaped through this wall to the next room
- Prop 5: Shih-na was in the adjacent room but couldn't see into the escape room
- Prop 9: The revolving fireplace wall is between Shih-na's room and the escape room

**Step 3:** Apply the propositions to reveal the contradiction

According to Prop 4 and Evidence 29, Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall. However, Shih-na's Testimony 3 claims she was "in the next room" but couldn't see Yatagarasu. This creates a contradiction because:

- If Shih-na was truly in the adjacent room (as she claims in Testimony 3), and Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall into that same room (as stated in Evidence 29), then Shih-na should have been able to see Yatagarasu entering her room
- The spatial arrangement shows the revolving fireplace wall connects the two rooms, making it impossible for someone in the adjacent room to miss someone entering through that wall

**Step 4:** The contradiction is revealed through spatial reasoning

The physical layout established by the propositions shows that the revolving fireplace wall directly connects the two rooms. If Yatagarasu escaped through this wall into the room where Shih-na claims to be, she would have necessarily seen this happen. Her testimony that she "was unable to catch a glimpse" directly contradicts the physical evidence of the escape route.
